ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u23	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Michał Jach, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u24	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the governmental draft law on special solutions concerning the municipality of Ostrowice in the West Pomeranian Voivodeship, prints No. 2650 and 2666. Ladies and gentlemen, we have a precedent in Poland. The Act abolishes the municipality of Ostrowice. This is the result of the extreme pathology of local government, which led to such a catastrophic situation of the municipality, that the only solution that does not condemn the inhabitants to life without the future is the liquidation of this municipality. This situation has two aspects: self-government, local, and government, central. For many years in this municipality is ruled by Wójt Wacław Micewski, who led to such scandalous debt of the municipality that the debt currently exceeds 4.5 times the annual income of this municipality. But the second aspect is also important here, the government, because already in 2010 the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration received an official letter from the Regional Audit Chamber in Szczecin informing about the extremely difficult budgetary situation of the municipality, which was eligible for the appointment of a Commissioner. Unfortunately, neither last year nor in the following years the government of the Civic Platform and the Polish People's Party did anything to prevent this situation in any way. It is worth noting that since 2010 this debt has increased, it can be said, in geometric progress and in the municipality - not as the only case but as a characteristic case - it has been indebted not in banks, but in parabanks. This has led to such a state that the only solution that will allow residents to look forward with optimism, and the areas of this municipality to develop and plan the future, is only liquidation. This is a precedent, but it is also worth asking for reflection for the inhabitants of Poland. We're in the pre-election period before the elections to local government. It is worth looking at our municipalities, our districts, and thinking about how those who represent us in these authorities are working, whether or not they deserve another term of office. Law and Justice fully supports the government project. I think this is the optimal solution for residents of Ostrowice. We believe that the municipalities that take over Ostrowice will also have a chance to develop. Your marshal, on behalf of the club, I'm making amends. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u25	Thank you. Please take the floor of Mr Mark Hok, the Civic Platform.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u26	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the draft law on special solutions concerning the municipality of Ostrowice in the Zachodniopomorskie voivodship. Ostrowice Municipality, as we all know, is one of the most indebted municipalities in Poland. The Regional Audit Chamber in Szczecin has given the opinion that the municipality has no financial perspectives to carry out investments for many years, which shows that there is no possibility of economic development. I do not want to go back historically to how it happened, because Mr Jach clearly pointed out the guilty. The guilt was very multifactorial and distributed. In 2015, voivode Marek Tałasiewicz applied for the abolition of the commune. It was only after 2 years that what we are discussing today happened, i.e. a law on special solutions has been drawn up. Of course, the aim of the project was primarily to create regulations aimed at the liquidation of Ostrowice Municipality, the abolition of Ostrowice Municipality without primarily transferring to the municipality the burdens associated with the operation and repayment of the municipal liabilities, as well as the determination of the volume of the commune's liabilities and the procedure for the settlement of claims. The liabilities of the municipality of Ostrowice arising before its abolition will be borne by the State Treasury, while the debts of the municipality of Ostrowice will become liabilities of the municipality to which the area will be included. And this is actually good information for local authorities who want to take over the commune of Ostrowice, i.e. the communes of Złocieniec and Drawsko Pomorskie. But there is also bad information, because in our opinion and in the opinion of local authorities, ensuring the normal functioning of the abolished commune in the new reality will generate further great costs, which they cannot even think about and dream about at the moment, according to this law, local authorities. Namely, the entry in perpetual books concerning change of owners is the cost of tens of thousands on the part of new local governments. The employment of new workers and the increase in the scope of tasks of existing workers resulting from the merger of parts of Ostrowice Municipality will lead to an extension of the scope of activities carried out by the employees. Costs include: checks, additional annual salaries for employees, maintenance of the building in which the Ostrowice Municipal Office is currently located, equipment and adaptation of office premises, specialised legal and financial services related to the acquisition process, adaptation of the IT base, notification of changes in borders, new tax authorities, administrative authorities, bank accounts, documents related to changes in local law, including planning documents, and finally launching a coherent public collective transport in the enlarged municipality. In our view, therefore, and in the opinion of the local authorities, it is necessary to propose an amendment which would somehow compensate for these expenses. The amendment concerns Article. Article 41 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Act on the Income of Local Government Entities and the inclusion in the proposed Act of the provision that for municipalities resulting from the merger of two or more municipalities by means of concerted resolutions, the ratio of participation in income tax revenues from individuals as defined in Article. 4, for a period of 5 years, from 1 January of the year following the year in which the merger decision was taken, would be increased by 5 percentage points. Ladies and gentlemen, this is the scale of PLN 3 million per year. The amount of PLN 3 million will certainly not destroy the state budget, and local authorities who want to take over this divided municipality will be able to improve the infrastructure of this neglected municipality of Ostrowice in a responsible manner. It is also the answer to the residents that it will not only be from the budget of their municipalities, from their taxes, but also expenditure will be on the side of the Treasury. That is why we will make this amendment. Finally, as regards the duration of this liquidation process, that during the year when two government commissioners changed, this debt increased by another PLN 10 million. If this bill had been prepared faster and more efficiently, we would have been, I think, in a different situation. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will support the bill, but amends the 5% equivalent for municipalities. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u46	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Minister, I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Energy and the Treasury on the Government's draft law amending the Law on the Fuel Quality Monitoring and Control System and the Act on the National Tax Administration. Commission for Energy and Treasury at its meeting on 25 April took note of this draft law. During the work of the committee, five amendments were tabled by the Legislative Office of an editorial and legislative nature. The amendments tabled were accepted by the committee. As we read in the explanatory memorandum, the essential effect of the entry into force of the proposed amendment of the Law of 25 August 2006 on the monitoring and control system for the quality of fuels will be the monitoring and control of the quality of solid fuels in accordance with the state of technical knowledge and experience in their application, with particular reference to the reduction of emissions of pollutants and greenhouse gases and other substances. The proposed changes will also bring the solid fuel market into order and enable consumers to buy solid fuels that meet the quality requirements for fuels intended for the municipal and residential sectors. Undoubtedly, the most important objective of this law is to improve air quality by eliminating the worst quality solid fuels from the sale. The Supreme Chamber of Control has been working on air quality in Poland for many years, publishing reports on this subject. The first report was issued in 2000. It is a report informing about the need to regulate two fundamental issues in the legal system, in order to improve air quality in Poland significantly. These issues concern the introduction of quality standards for solid fuels and the introduction of emission standards for solid fuel boilers. The bill discussed today meets these expectations. The most important changes contained in the bill can be considered among others. the definition of combustion plants in which solid fuels covered by the Act are used. The Act concerns solid fuels burning in households and combustion plants with a rated thermal input of less than 1 MW. The Act amends and extends the catalogue of solid fuels. According to the Act, solid fuel is among others. coal, briquettes or pellets containing a minimum specified percentage of hard coal. The Act introduces an obligation to use a quality certificate which is passed on to each sale of solid fuel. This is information for the buyer about what he buys and what's in the solid fuel product. The Act prohibits the sale and import to the municipal and domestic sector of fuels which do not comply with the requirements of the quality regulation. The Act gives the authority to issue relevant regulations on the quality of solid fuels, methods of testing the quality of solid fuels, the method of sampling solid fuels, the model introduced and the quality certificate which organises and certify the type of coal in the Polish market. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! At its meeting on 25 April this year, the Energy and Treasury Commission unanimously adopted the draft law, together with the amendments made. I propose that the draft law and the solutions proposed therein be adopted together with the amendments adopted by the committee. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u48	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I am pleased to present my position on the governmental draft law amending the Law on the Fuel Quality Monitoring and Control System and the Act on National Tax Administration in print Nos 2377 and 2537. The main objective of this revision is to improve air quality by eliminating the lowest quality solid fuels from the market. As part of the work at government level and during the committee's deliberations, optimal solutions were adopted. They will, on the one hand, allow a decisive improvement in the quality of fuels used by citizens and, on the other hand, will not impose disproportionate obligations and restrictions on them. The adoption of amendments to the Fuel Quality Monitoring and Control Act is undoubtedly a major step forward in the fight against smog. We must remember that on 22 February this year. The Court of Justice of the European Union issued a judgment in which it concluded that the Republic of Poland exceeded the limit value for PM10 dust concentration in the years 2007 to 2015. Poland was also accused of abandoning appropriate measures to improve air quality. The Court has obliged the authorities of the Republic of Poland to take appropriate action in this respect. This amendment is part of Poland's implementation of the Court's recommendations. By the way, it is also worth mentioning that the Supreme Audit Chamber in 2000 and 2014 has repeatedly drawn attention to a serious problem with air quality in Poland and pointed out solutions that should be implemented. Once again, it is due to the actions of the United Right Government that we are finally working on a project that solves this problem at national level. The solutions proposed in the Act are primarily provisions authorising the Minister responsible for energy to issue relevant regulations governing a number of key issues in terms of fuel quality. In this way they will be defined, among others. requirements for the quality of solid fuels and methods for testing their quality. The projects mentioned above the regulations were prepared during the Ministry and Government work on the amendment and attached to the project itself. As a result, all interested persons had the opportunity to get to know them in good time. Further changes included in the Act include, among others, the definition of combustion plants in which solid fuels covered by the Act are used. The Act concerns solid fuels burning in households and combustion plants with a rated thermal input of less than 1 MW. Further issues include the change and extension of the solid fuel catalogue. According to the Act, solid fuel is among others. hard coal, briquettes or pellets containing at least a certain percentage of hard coal. The Act also introduces an obligation to use a quality certificate which is passed on to each sale of solid fuels. The regulation also contains provisions prohibiting the sale and import of fuels which do not comply with the requirements of the quality regulation to the municipal and residential sector. Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I would like to express my great joy that we are already at the end of the parliamentary phase of work on such an important and expected law. I would also like to thank all those involved in this project: representatives of the government, members of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury and representatives of all the organisations and companies involved in the project. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u50	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present the club's position regarding the report of the Energy and Treasury Commission on the government bill amending the Act on the Fuel Quality Monitoring and Control System and the Act on National Tax Administration, prints no. 2377 and 2537. The bill entered the Sejm on 19 March 2018 and was addressed to work in the Committee on Energy and the Treasury, where the first reading took place on 25 April. High Izbo! It should be stressed here that during the meeting of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury at which this reading took place - despite the prior notification to the secretariat of the committee - Marshal of the Sejm did not agree to the admission of invited guests, including among others. representatives of the coal mining hubs, business representatives and experts, except those who have notified the Ministry of Energy. Such a forced form of silent parliamentary debate by the Marshal of the Sejm is clearly distorting the legislative process, preventing the social side and all interested parties from discussing the law-making process. The main effect of the proposed amendment of this law, as indicated in the explanatory memorandum, will be the monitoring and control of the quality of solid fuels, in accordance with the state of technical knowledge and experience in their application, with particular reference to the reduction of emissions of pollutants and greenhouse gases and other substances. Another effect of the proposed solution will be to organise the solid fuel market and enable consumers to buy solid fuels that meet the quality requirements for fuels intended for the municipal and residential sectors. Interestingly, the legislator withdrew in the explanatory memorandum of the bill from the other effect it would have had after the entry into force of the Act and which it presented at the Parliamentary Mining and Energy Team meeting on 21 February 2017, i.e. the effect of reducing imports of coal from third countries outside the European Union. It is necessary, High Izbo, to make it clear that this law on the carriage of coal from European Union countries does not impose any restrictions, because imported coal is enough to be transported through the territory of another EU country so that there are no obligations and restrictions related to the placing on the market procedure. The most important changes contained in the Act are, first of all, the definition of combustion plants in which solid fuels covered by the Act are used - the Act concerns solid fuels burning in households and combustion installations with a nominal heat output of less than 1 MW; the amendment and extension of the catalogue of solid fuels; the introduction of an obligation to use a certificate of quality and to prohibit imports, sales to the municipal and domestic sectors of fuels which do not comply with the requirements of the Quality Regulation - coal mules, flotoconcentrates, brown coal, fuel from any blending of solid fuels containing less than 85% hard coal; introduction of penalties and fines for traders who will sell solid fuel contrary to the law and the quality requirements of the regulation. The Act also provides the Minister for Energy with tools which allow him to introduce, by regulation, for a period of not more than 60 days, the possibility of temporarily withdrawing from the quality standards for solid fuels. There is here, High Izbo, a threat to which, as Members of the opposition, we pointed out at first reading, that the failure to implement production plans in domestic coal producers, which we have faced over the past 2 years in the Polish Mining Group and other coal companies, may be an excuse for the Minister of Energy to allow the introduction of coal which does not comply with this law to the market. High Izbo! The Act is part of the actions already undertaken by the local government in the six voivodship seymics, which implemented the so-called. Anti-smog resolutions aimed at eliminating from the household heating market poor quality fuels and thus reducing low emissions and improving the health of our citizens. However, it should be pointed out here that the concern of the PiS government about the future of Polish mining is unfortunately fake. This is confirmed by the government's decision to close further mines and mining plants over the last 2.5 years, a permanent liquidation of about 10 thousand. jobs in mining, and hence in the mining industry. Imports of coal to Poland in 2017 increased by as much as 65% compared to the previous year and amounted to over 13 million tons of coal, of which almost 9 million tons from Russia. High Izbo! To sum up my speech, I would like to inform the High House that the Civic Platform Club will vote in favour of the adoption of this law in order to improve air quality in Poland, in the interests of the health and life of citizens who have the right to breathe clean air. However, we expect the PiS government to make real proposals to reduce the financial burden on almost 5.5 million households, which will have to pay more for heating their homes, which Mr Tobiszowski announced at the meeting of the Energy and Treasury Commission. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u53	Thank you. Mr Mirosław Suchoń, the Modern Club, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u54	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Poselski Club on the government proposal for a law amending the law on the system of monitoring and control of the quality of fuels and the Act on National Tax Administration. Ladies and gentlemen! The aim of the proposed law and implementing acts is, as has been said several times here, to improve air quality and to eliminate from the sale of solid fuels of worse, worst quality, and it should be said here openly and explicitly that this does not apply to the whole spectrum of combustion and to the communal-byte sector. Indeed, this affects what air we breathe every day, but it is not all fuel burning in all the installations that exist in Poland. In fact, the purpose of the law in particular is precisely to define these installations, specific combustion plants in which fuels covered by the law are used. According to the project presented to the High House, these fuels will be examined precisely for households and combustion plants with a rated thermal input of less than 1 MW. In addition, the Act amends and extends the catalogue of solid fuels. According to the Act it will be among others. hard coal, briquettes or pellets containing a certain percentage of hard coal. Products withdrawn from the market shall also be indicated. In addition, the Act introduces an obligation to use a quality certificate, which is passed on when selling coal, which will be important for customers who, when purchasing, will be able to check what are the quality parameters of the coal they buy and the fuels they buy. Moreover, what is important, the law prohibits sales, imports into the municipal and domestic sector of fuels which do not comply with the requirements of the quality regulation. Well, that's an important record, because if we look at it, for example. on burning coals such as mules and flotation concentrates, as we see in the justification, they cause a high emission of total suspended dust, including subfractions PM10 and PM2,5, which is of course the most important for the health of Poles. Also brown coal causes about five times higher emissions of dust during combustion, as well as many times higher emissions of these substances, which are the most harmful. From this point of view, such records are most desirable. As a result of these consultations in the Ministry of Development, it was considered appropriate that we submit this bill for notification, and after we started our first reading, so we started the procession, a speech was addressed to the European Commission. On the other hand, ladies and gentlemen, I would stop - two words - on the issue of imports. There were a few questions - and Mr Bańkowski and Mr Gadowski - about import growth. Yes, there is an increase in imports, and I will say that this is a certain concern of mine as the person responsible for the coal sector. But, ladies and gentlemen, what's going on? Yeah, we were all fed with information that we have too much coal on the Polish market. As you've noticed, there's been a cooling down of the sector for a few good years. And I don't want to argue with my predecessors here because I don't want to claim that someone's making mistakes, but let's see, ladies and gentlemen, we've had a very long period of coal production, too many mines, too many mines, too many mines, and then suddenly we've cooled down the mines. We have actually since 2017, when we started managing the sector - because in 2016 it was a rescue, it was a fire extinguisher - since 2017 we have spent more than 1,200 million for the Polish Mining Group for individual years, probably by 2022, for investments, in order to start on the one hand a qualitatively better coal, and on the other hand to respond to the demand for coal. We have a parametric programme on Polish energy and I think we will discuss it this year. There we predict the role of the Polish coal sector by 2050 - 50% of the raw material of hard coal, brown coal in the Polish energy space. And this must be answered in the first place by Polish mining, as well as the entire heating system, which, turns out, is powered by coal from the outside. That's why this year is very important to us, because it actually appeared to us quite significant, I admit, an increase in the amount of carbon from the outside. But what does he show? He, too - this is a bad phenomenon - is a very strong incentive for us in a situation where we have COP this year to show that we want to cool down and we have no plans, Mr Bańkowski, to eliminate mines, and please do not discuss this anymore. It is at my heart that we launch a new mine in the Polish Mining Group, because we need coal, and as far as this is concerned, this is explained. Today we have a challenge, ladies and gentlemen. We've got an analysis from the outside of 13.14 million, ba, 15, we've done an analysis for last year. This shows that there is a market for coal in Poland. As there is a market for coal in Poland, we should use it and not discuss whether to invest in Polish coal, because then we leave this pension related to the profit of importing coal to other players in Europe or outside Europe, but in part of the world. I think it is very important that we be aware that Poland needs coal, without discussing whether one or another government is talking about it because the market dictates it to us. Hence these investments, which are very important to us, especially as regards the Polish Mining Group, so that we can increase the amount of coal on the Polish market. But as you know, in mining, we know today whether we know about last year... There's no way in a year or a half a year to run the coal deposits we'd like. We also have a problem with companies in the environment that have been mutilated by the crisis, and they also rebuild as those that will not always start in tenders. And that's also a big challenge for us. Ba, we also rebuild management staff and staff in individual companies. As regards Mrs Kaczorowski's question, the question concerned the issue of low emissions. The environmental effect of eliminating coal mules, Commissioner, will above all be to reduce emissions of all dusts. This is very important. When a mule is replaced by a medium-quality carbon fuel, dust emissions will be reduced two and a half times when carbon mule is replaced by so-called carbon mule. better coal or ecogrosh, or solid fuel of good quality, dust emissions will be reduced almost seven times. So this is the concrete effect of this bill when it comes to clean air. As regards Mrs Kozanecka's question, you asked about the ban on the use of low-quality fuels in industry, why this law does not provide for it. The ban on low-quality fuels will not include industry and energy, i.e. installations above 1 MW of nominal heat output, because we have installations that are ecologically capable of burning these coals, which is why we only included installations up to 1 MW. As regards Mrs Glenc's question, it was a question of the real cost of implementing this law. The costs for the State Treasury were calculated by the UOKiK on the basis of its long experience with controls, the costs for producers were estimated by the Ministry of Energy. As I mentioned, investment outlays are among others. to compensate for the loss of 50 million per balance in sales on low quality coals. Through investment outlays we go into better prices when it comes to coal, and here we are able to compensate ourselves for this price. Of course, the challenge is for us to be able to compensate where these coals are more expensive, and I am about to say about the Clean Air programme, how we are going to compensate for whether we are trying to compensate for the rise in coal prices when it comes to individual combustion. Mr Matusiak asked what the economic consequences for more than 10 000 activities would be. Polish coal trading companies following the entry into force of the Act amending the Act on the Fuel Quality Monitoring System. Manufacturers and intermediaries who offer solid fuels for the household market will be required to use fuel quality certificates. This will enable the buyer to obtain reliable and comprehensive information about the product. We proceed with the examination of item 3 of the agenda: Report from the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development on the Government's draft law amending the Act on the fight against unfair competition and certain other laws (Prints No. 2549 and 2612). I ask Mr Wojciech Murdzk to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u96	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Report of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development on the Government's draft law amending the act on combating unfair competition and certain other laws, print No 2549. The Sejm at its 63rd meeting on 7 June 2018 pursuant to Article 7 of the Rules of Procedure. 39 (1) (a) 2 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development for consideration. The Committee on Economic Affairs and Development, after considering this draft law at its meeting on 14 June, presents a proposal to the High House for further proceedings. The purpose of the proposed law is to transpose into Polish law the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 8 June 2016 on the protection of classified information: the information of a company and classified commercial information - the secrets of the company - from their unlawful acquisition, use and disclosure. The Directive summarises nearly five years of work carried out by European Union bodies in order to harmonise the rules on standards for the protection of confidential business information. In the light of its objectives, the Directive aims to establish a minimum standard for the protection of confidential business information as a tool for managing competitiveness and innovation. The draft law consists of amendments to the law on combating unfair competition, amendments to the Code of Civil Procedure, amendments to the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection, as well as transitional and final provisions. The main issues that are trying to correlate this with the European Parliament directive are: defining information protected as a company's secret, i.e. above all, to determine the scope of the measure in question; to identify actions relating to the secret of an undertaking which constitute an act of unfair competition (transfer, disclosure and use) in various aspects of the phenomenon; to determine the penalties provided for in cases of unfair competition in respect of the secret of the undertaking. The amendments to the Civil Law Code relate primarily to the scope of the protection proceedings and issues relating to the bail for the protection of claims. In the case of amendments to the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection, it is primarily a precise definition of the company's secrecy prepared for the purposes of the Act. After a detailed examination of the draft, the committee asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u97	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Andrzej Gawron, Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u98	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice regarding the government draft law amending the Act on Combating Unfair Competition and some other laws. The aim of the project is to transpose into the Polish legal order the 2006 European Parliament and Council Directive No 943 on the protection of the scope of classified technical knowledge and commercially classified information (the secrets of enterprises) against their unlawful acquisition, use and disclosure. This Directive aims to establish a minimum standard for the protection of confidential information. The solutions adopted shall determine the European level of protection of classified commercial information. This is necessary in view of the fact that the current rules discourage businesses from taking cross-border action in view of the different levels of protection of their secrets in different countries. In the longer term, the proposed solutions are intended to improve the functioning of the common market. In view of the specific nature of the subject-matter of the amendment, the provisions of this Act cover several legal acts, i.e. the law on combating unfair competition, the Code of Civil Procedure and the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection. It should be emphasized that in Poland we have a long, 90-year tradition of protecting the secrets of the company, and for 20 years in our legal order there has been a recognition of an unlawful violation of the company's secrecy as an act of unfair competition. Adapting EU solutions does not require transposition in the form of a completely new law and can be carried out on the basis of the laws already in force. We are implementing a number of amendments to the Act on Combating Unfair Competition as a basic act. We introduce, among others. the definition of the company's secret which constitutes the transposition of Article. 1 and 5 directives. We then implement civil law solutions, which will make it easier for operators to exercise their rights in the event of a breach of the company's secrecy. It is worth noting that we are already applying the legal solutions required by Article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 Directives. It's about an anti-executive action, Art. 840 point 2 of the Civil Procedure Code, and an action for determination, Article. 189 Code of Civil Procedure. This is evidence of a lack of necessity, as I mentioned earlier, the introduction of an additional, completely new law. Changes in the C.P.C. include changes in the protection proceedings, Article. 739 § 1 of the Code of Civil Procedure, on bail against claims of persons other than those under obligation. In accordance with this amendment, the situation of other persons whose rights, as appropriate, may be infringed as a result of the enforcement of the freezing order will be treated on an equal footing with the situation in which the freezing order interferes. Additional mechanisms have also been put in place to secure possible claims and to secure the creditor. The amendments to the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection are purely technical and are a consequence of a change in the definition of company secrecy. Overall, the draft regulation aims to enhance the protection of the company's secrecy, which is technical, technological and organisational information. It is worth noting that the changes implemented will benefit the sector of small and medium-sized enterprises. According to the preamble to the implementing directive, the rules on the protection of business secrets are of greater importance to small and medium-sized enterprises than to other market participants. These solutions should contribute to an increase in the competitiveness of the economy. The Law and Justice Club supports the project presented. We believe that it should be adopted as soon as possible, especially as time limits apply here. On behalf of the club, I submit five technical amendments to the draft. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u106	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! The draft amendment of the law adapts Polish law to European Union rules on the protection of classified know-how and commercially classified information, in short, goes towards the protection of business secrets. They will be subject to stricter penalties and the entity that benefits from disclosure will have to pay compensation equal to the licence fee. Currently, national rules in the European Union create an unequal level of protection of business secrets. These differences may discourage entrepreneurs from taking up activities of an international nature, particularly in an area requiring investment in innovation. The project proposes e.g. clarification of the definition of company secrecy - mainly its form and availability - and the introduction of new legal protection measures in the event of breach of company secrecy. These include the publication of a judgment or information on its content, the settlement of remuneration for the use of the company's secrecy in the future and the determination of compensation as the equivalent of the licence fee due for the exercise of the company's secrecy. Ladies and gentlemen! The amended provisions also introduce criminal liability for persons who use or disclose the secret of an undertaking that they have become aware of in connection with their participation in proceedings relating to an act of unfair competition or in connection with the reading of an act of such proceedings, in so far as the Court of First Instance has ruled out the disclosure of the case. This will apply to all participants in the proceedings: parties, representatives, experts, translators, members of the formation of the court, minutesmen, employees of the Registry of the court, as well as persons who, without being participants in the proceedings, have obtained access to the file, e.g. in connection with visiting activities. The new solutions will allow participants in the European Union's single internal market to benefit from the same protection of business secrecy. This should, on the one hand, discourage unfair competition and, on the other hand, create a legal framework for the use of know-how. This should make the European Union a more competitive market. Ladies and gentlemen! Therefore, the club of the Polish People's Party - the European Union of Democrats will support the changes being made, acting on behalf of and for the benefit of entrepreneurs. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The draft law which is currently in second reading is the first act which sets out the principles of the functioning of the national cybersecurity system in general, but does not regulate the issues already identified in other laws. It can be estimated that the law correctly states the state cybersecurity system. It seems that if it is meticulously implemented, it will create the conditions for security in the digital space. Conceived performance of tasks under the Act will be the responsibility of dozens of entities, which may pose a problem in ensuring the integrity of the system. A catalogue of institutions that can be made available on request from the list of key service operators has been extended to include a new entity recently appointed, i.e. the State Protection Service. The provisions on CSIRT recommendations for the use of IT devices or software have been amended so that they are strictly embedded in the concepts relevant to the project in question. The project was supplemented by the necessary amendments to the Internal Security Agency and the Intelligence Agency Act, in particular the ABW has been given new powers to operate and coordinate an early warning system on the Internet. The project was complemented by the necessary amendments to the Public Procurement Law, in particular the grounds for rejection for public security reasons, namely that it is also a question of the security of entities included in the single list of critical infrastructure facilities, installations, facilities and services. The government text also introduced changes of order and editorial nature, which is a routine case. On the other hand, the committee disagreed with some of the proposals made by Members. She didn't agree, among other things. to carry out every 4 years a security audit of the information system for the provision of a key service, instead of every 2 years as established in the government project. This change was not justified, this possible change was unjustified due to rapid technological change and an audit at a 4-year rate - this is too long. The Commission has not made a proposed amendment concerning the extension of the deadlines to 6 and 12 months respectively, which the government project set for 3 and 6 months, to fulfil specific tasks of key service operators. Key service operators are obliged to protect their systems and fulfil their obligations under the Act as soon as possible. And such an extension of the deadline was unjustified. The Commission also refused to extend the 24-hour deadline for reporting a serious and significant incident to 48 hours. Information on these incidents shall be provided immediately. Of course, this is due to cybersecurity considerations. Parts of the amendments have not been taken into account due to their obvious, inappropriate nature. It was proposed, for example. the addition of a provision enabling the use of the so-called sector-specific IT systems by being able to connect to the central electronic system. It is clear that such operations can be carried out without specific provisions. Nor was it agreed to add a provision on the possibility for sector-specific cybersecurity teams to coordinate educational campaigns, as such campaigns can already be carried out today, nowadays, and this does not require legislation. In conclusion, it should be noted that, given the importance of this new regulation, it should be adopted as soon as possible. Thank you very much.

Addressing the need to strengthen coordination and exchange of information in the strategic and political layer between the institutions responsible for cybersecurity in the civilian, military, key service sectors and the institutions responsible for combating cybercrime, and given that existing activities have been dispersed, the projector assumes the appointment of a government representative for cybersecurity. According to Art. 62 The representative of the government for cybersecurity will be responsible for coordinating the implementation of cybersecurity tasks at national level in the Republic of Poland. A representative of the Government, in the rank of Secretary of State or Undersecretary of State, shall be appointed and dismissed by the President of the Council of Ministers. His tasks will include: analysis and evaluation of the functioning of the national cybersecurity system on the basis of aggregated data and indicators developed with the involvement of state administrations, competent authorities and CSIRT teams. The Cybersecurity Strategy of the Republic of Poland referred to in Chapter 13 will be used in the implementation of this process. An element of the strategy is the approach to risk assessment, which will take the form of a cybersecurity risk management methodology developed under the Strategy Action Plan. The government's representative will also be responsible for the opinion of draft legislation and other government documents affecting the implementation of cybersecurity tasks, as well as the initiation of national cybersecurity exercises. The draft law also provides for the establishment of a Cyber Security College as an advisory and advisory body on planning, monitoring and coordinating the activities of CSIRT teams, sectoral cybersecurity teams and competent authorities - says Article. 64. The need to create an advisory body was signalled during the agreement and evaluation of the project with the entities that will form part of the system. The activities of the college will ensure greater consistency and transparency of the system, and will give the issue of cybersecurity an appropriate rank and enable the formulation of coherent orientations and plans to address cyber security threats. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u143	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present our position on the government draft law amending the law on the organisation of the market in milk and milk products contained in print No 2547. The amendment of the Law of 20 April 2004 on the organisation of the market in milk and milk products aims at identifying one entity - the National Agricultural Support Centre, as appropriate in matters relating to the submission by milk collectors of monthly information on the quantity of milk delivered to them each month. Currently, these matters, with the exception of penalties imposed by the Agency for the restructuring and modernisation of agriculture for failure to provide this information, are being dealt with by the NOWR. The draft provides that the fines to be imposed on milk collectors for failure to provide information to the NOWR on the cessation of their milk purchasing activities, currently imposed by the ARIMR, shall be imposed by the relevant NOWR units. The draft law in question also takes into account the provisions of the Act of 10 February 2017. The provisions introducing the Act on the National Agricultural Support Centre on the basis of which the NOWR carries out public intervention tasks as delegated tasks by the ARiMR, according to which ARiMR develops all documentation related to this scope. In addition, the draft law clarifies the local jurisdiction of the authorities authorised to carry out checks on producer organisations and their associations, interbranch organisations and milk collectors. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 3 of the draft law, the transitional provisions regulate the issues arising from the designation of a new body in matters relating to the imposition of such fines. It was pointed out that it was initiated before the date of entry into force of the contested law and not completed by final decision of the proceedings on fines for failure to provide the information referred to in Article. 2a The laws on the organisation of the market in milk and milk products, carried out by the President of the ARiMR and the Regional Branch Director of the ARiMR, shall, as from the date of entry into force of the Act under consideration today, carry out, in accordance with their jurisdiction, the Director-General of KOWR and the Director-General of the KOWR Field Branch. The other amendments are of a more precise or technical nature. The obligation on NEBR to impose fines for the failure of milk collectors to provide monthly information on the quantity of milk bought in does not require the guarantee of additional staffing in NEBR. The current provisions provide that the NKER shall carry out checks on milk collectors for the transfer of the abovementioned. information. The imposition of penalties shall be the consequence of the checks being carried out. In addition, it should be stressed that the number of decisions concerning the imposition of fines for this purpose by the Agricultural Market Agency, which was authorised to monitor and impose fines in this respect before 1 September 2017, was negligible. The proposed changes will result, among others. the abolition of the rules on the division of competences between NKER and ARiMR in respect of monthly milk collectors' information on the quantity of milk bought into NKR, i.e. ensuring that the fines for failure to provide monthly information on concentrated milk are met by the NOWR, which collects that information on the basis of the rules in force, carries out checks on milk collectors to that extent and is authorised to forward that information to the European Commission. As regards the imposition of fines by the NEBR on milk collectors for failure to provide information to the Director-General of NEBR on the cessation of their milk purchasing activities, this will ensure that penalties are imposed on milk collectors by one operator. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice is in favour of the bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u147	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Minister, The proposed rules are designed to regulate the division of competences covering monthly information provided by milk collectors on the quantity of concentrated milk between the National Agricultural Support Centre and the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture. In accordance with the Act of 10 February 2017 on the National Agricultural Support Centre, which entered into force on 1 September 2017, the KOWR took over the tasks of the Agricultural Market Agency. Since then, milk collectors shall communicate monthly information on the quantity of milk bought into KOWR. However, so far, the authority authorised to impose fines for failure to provide monthly information by milk collectors has been the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture. This authorisation will now also be taken over by the KOWR and this is a logical change because it is the KOWR who is required to control the milk collectors in order to comply with the obligation to provide information in a given month. Documentation on the extent of buying-in of milk will therefore be collected in one place. In addition, the National Agricultural Support Centre is obliged to provide the European Commission with aggregated information on the quantity of milk bought up in a given month. The European Commission's assessment shows that the number of dairy cows will decrease by 0.4% in 2018 while their milk yield will rise by 1.7%. The costs of buying the feed which the farmer lost as a result of the drought may be entered there. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u159	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy, contained in print No. 2627, on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Social Security, print No. 2588. On 5 June 2018, the Marshal of the Sejm sent the draft law in print No 2588 to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs for first reading. The Commission, after first reading and considering this draft law at its meeting on 13 June 2018, asks: The High Sejm to adopt a draft law on printing No 2588. The aim of the project is to define and organise the rules for the placement of the Demographic Reserve Fund from the redemption of the assets referred to in Article. Article 58 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 point 4a of the Social Insurance Act, which is part of the Demographic Reserve Fund portfolio. According to the draft law, measures carried over from OFE are proposed to remove the evidentiary separation of assets referred to in Article. Article 58 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 point 4a of the Social Insurance Act, repeal of the provisions - Art. 59 (1) (a) 1 point 3 of the Social Insurance Act - providing that the funds and revenues derived from the assets referred to in Article. Article 58 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 point 4a of the above Act may be used only for the provision of the pension fund, the introduction of the principle that the funds deriving from the redemption of those assets and the revenue derived from them would increase the assets of the Demographic Reserve Fund and would be classified and managed under the other assets of the Demographic Reserve Fund. As regards the funds deriving from the disposal in the context of privatisation of shares and shares belonging to the Treasury, it is proposed to repeal the provision of the Article. 63. 3 restricting the instruments in which the measures referred to in Article may be placed. Article 58 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 point 2 of the Social Security Act. It is proposed to abolish the investment restrictions on the funds deriving from the disposal in the framework of the privatisation of shares and shares belonging to the State Treasury which have been transferred to the Demographic Reserve Fund and to enable these funds to be placed on a general basis. In addition, it is proposed to introduce a provision in the Social Insurance Act laying down the way the Social Insurance Institution collects fees for the obligation to collect and collect health insurance contributions from the National Health Fund to the Labour Fund and the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund and to open pension funds. The undertaking shall collect the costs incurred as proposed in the form of a deduction from the payments made. It is proposed that the regulation should enter into force on 23 October 2018, with the exception of the provision of Article. Point 1 (5) which would have entered into force within 14 days of the date of publication. The deadline of 23 October would allow the Demographic Reserve Fund to allow funds received on 24 October 2018 from the redemption of IDS1018 bonds to be placed on new terms similar to those for the other Demographic Reserve Fund. On the other hand, the provision of the article. Point 1 (5) is merely a provision in the Social Security Act. The adoption of the proposed solutions will harmonise and simplify the rules for the location of the Demographic Reserve Fund. The bill will not require any costs on the part of the Social Insurance Institution and the Demographic Reserve Fund. The Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy, without opposition and without amendment, adopted the draft law report in print 2627. Article 58 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 point 4a of the Social Insurance Act, which according to the provisions of the Act are recorded. The draft law proposes the elimination of the registration separation in the Demographic Reserve Fund portfolio of funds resulting from the transfer of assets from OFE. One of the objectives of the arrangements provided for in this Act is to enable the Demographic Reserve Fund to locate funds from the assets of the fund arising from the transfer of assets from the OFE on a similar basis to that of other funds of the Fund. The placement of funds from the Demographic Reserve Fund's assets resulting from the transfer of assets from open pension funds in accordance with uniform principles and equivalent limitations as for other assets of the Fund will ensure comparable security of these funds and the achievement of the Fund's objectives in subsequent years. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u162	Thank you very much. Mrs Marzena Okła-Drewnowicz, Civic Platform.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u163	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! We know that the purpose of the bill amending the Social Security Act is to lay down rules for the placement of the Demographic Reserve Fund from the redemption of assets included in the Demographic Reserve Fund portfolio, which according to the provisions of the Act are recorded. According to the Law of December 2013 on the amendment of certain laws in connection with the establishment of rules for the payment of pensions from funds collected in open pension funds, there has been a transfer of the compulsory obligations of the State Treasury of part of pension rights insured from open pension funds, from OFE, to the Social Insurance Institution. In February 2014, part of the assets accumulated in open pension funds amounted to a total of more than 51%. settlement units recorded in the account of each member of the open pension fund as at 31 January 2014 to the Social Insurance Institution. At the same time, the value of the funds corresponding to the value of settlement units decommitted by OFE has been recorded on the sub-accounts of insured persons operated by the Social Insurance Institution. The composition of the portfolio of the Demographic Reserve Fund - i.e. balance sheet funds - is largely fiscal paper, about 74% which give an average annual yield of more than 3%. In comparison, interest rates for in-bank deposits up to one year are currently yielding profitability actually less than 1.5%. The annual fixed coupon on bonds is over 6%. In view of this relatively high amount of free funds obtained from the redemption of bonds and the market situation, i.e. low interest rates on the in-bank investment market and a reduced supply of Treasury securities issued by the Ministry of Finance, the Demographic Reserve Fund is interested in acquiring long-term securities with a State guarantee in order to achieve better results in the management of the funds entrusted than in the case of placing funds in bank deposits. We therefore understand the abolition of investment restrictions in the case of funds deriving from the disposal in the context of privatisation of shares and shares belonging to the State Treasury which have been transferred to the Demographic Reserve Fund. Ensuring a greater variety of financial instruments in which the Demographic Reserve Fund can be placed can ensure greater efficiency and profitability of investments. And that's what we agree to. We believe that allowing investment opportunities to be greater can help to increase resources as much as possible. However, we do not agree with the elimination of the registration separation in the Demographic Reserve Fund portfolio of funds resulting from the transfer of assets from open pension funds. The funds deriving from the redemption of these assets and the revenue generated from them increasing the assets of the Demographic Reserve Fund should be further recorded. One of the objectives of the solutions provided for in the proposed law is therefore to enable the Demographic Reserve Fund to locate funds from assets deriving from the transfer of assets from the OFE on a similar basis to the rules for the placement of other funds from the Demographic Reserve Fund. And that's what we agree to. However, we believe that the funds derived from these assets should be recorded separately, as has been the case so far, should be isolated. People, Polish citizens should know what is going on, about the proverbial zloty, with funds derived from assets from open pension funds. For this reason, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will abstain from voting on this bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u171	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Thank you very much for the positions of the clubs regarding the proposed amendment of the Social Security Act. I think it's worth starting a little bit from the end, i.e. from the objectives of the Demographic Reserve Fund. If we reach the Social Security Act... This is a provision which, as Mrs Tomaszewska mentioned, has been in force from the very beginning in its original form, i.e. from 1 January 1999 The objective of the Demographic Reserve Fund is to raise funds to compensate for the shortfalls of the Social Insurance Fund, that is, the fund from which our pensions are financed directly due to demographic reasons. It is precisely these phenomena, which are mainly linked to demographic changes which cause a deterioration of relations between the generation of people paying social security contributions, i.e. people insured today and the generation of people receiving pension benefits. With regard to the origin of this draft law discussed today, it is related to the amendment of the law made under the Act of 6 December 2013 on the amendment of certain laws in connection with the definition of the rules for the payment of pensions from funds collected in open pension funds. Let me remind you that one of the basic principles of this law was the remission of 51.5% assets collected in open pension funds, saving the value of those assets on sub-accounts in the Social Insurance Institution and transferring, you can say, securities by OFE to the Social Insurance Institution. The problem is that some of the securities transferred to the Social Insurance Institution had a fairly distant maturity - it was not possible within such a time limit for most securities, i.e. In February 2014, the redemption of those securities only had to be postponed to maturity. The maturity of the bonds issued by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego to the National Road Fund was fixed at 24 October 2018. Hence the initiative discussed today to amend the Social Security Act. I think the numbers best represent the essence of the problem. As already discussed in the course of the discussion, the Demographic Reserve Fund today manages assets worth over PLN 25 billion. This is the amount that relates solely to balance sheet appropriations. Part of the funds managed by the Demographic Reserve Fund or under its responsibility remain outside the balance sheet. This is the value of PLN 11 700 million. This off-balance sheet part of the funds managed by the Social Insurance Institution and belonging to the Demographic Reserve Fund portfolio concerns this amendment. As regards the results of the investment activity of the Social Insurance Institution with regard to the funds collected in the Demographic Reserve Fund, this is a security-oriented policy. It can be said that this is a restriction on the freedom to place funds in those securities which are at the highest risk, primarily in shares. Today, in the investment portfolio of the Demographic Reserve Fund, about 74% are tax papers, these are safe tax papers. Their average annual profitability is 3,15% . If we compare this with the return on investments in interbank deposits - which today give a return rate of around 1,5% - that we have, you can say, the essence of the problem. 24 October 2018 is the date of redemption of the bonds I mentioned, i.e. the National Road Fund bonds. The nominal value of these bonds is 8 818 661 thousand. zł. If it were not for the law or the draft law discussed today, these measures could only be placed on bank deposits, i.e. they could achieve a rate of return on investment, I think, comparable to the rate of return that we are currently dealing with, i.e. at around 1.5%. Just like you. you mentioned during the discussion, the annual coupon of the National Road Fund bonds, i.e. the level of interest per year, a figure of 6,25%, so it is an advantage incomparably greater than that which the Social Insurance Institution would have obtained if these funds had been placed on the bank's seat. This project envisages the abolition of the registration separation of measures. It therefore assumes that the funds deriving from the redemption of the National Road Fund bonds will be recorded and placed under the same conditions as the other funds managed by the Social Insurance Institution. So we want to achieve this effect that was mentioned here during the discussion, i.e. effect in the form of readability, clarity, full information on these results of investment activities and the level of capital or the value of funds managed by the Social Insurance Institution, owned or held by the Demographic Reserve Fund. As regards Mrs Tomaszewska's question, what was the investment policy of the Demographic Reserve Fund in previous years, as I mentioned, the starting point is what is now in the Demographic Reserve Fund, which is intended to compensate for the social insurance fund's demographic deficiencies. Let me repeat again: this is the amount of PLN 25 368 million in terms of the funds that are in the balance sheet of the Demographic Reserve Fund and PLN 11 700 million in terms of funds that are separately recorded. In total, we can say that this is the amount of PLN 37 billion. This is the state of the Demographic Reserve Fund as at 18 January 2018. If we go back to history, back to the previous years, you could say it wasn't that good. If we look at the expenses of the Demographic Reserve Fund from 2010 onwards, it can be said that these amounts were from PLN 2.5 billion - in 2014 such a envelope was allocated to supplement the pension fund deficit resulting from demographic reasons - up to PLN 7.5 billion - such a fund pool of the Demographic Reserve Fund was allocated, supported the Social Insurance Fund in the pension part in 2010. Neither in 2015 nor in 2016, in 2017, nor in the 2018 budget, was it intended to use or make use of the Demographic Reserve Fund for the provision of the pension fund, the Social Insurance Fund. Thank you. [[Applause]] [[Member Marzena Okła-Drewnowicz: To keep this bill separate...]] I have the impression that I have answered this question, Commissioner. I mean... High Izbo! In recent months, Poles have been concerned about media reports of burning waste dumps, cases of waste dumps, environmental pollution. It is understandable that these messages are of obvious concern. Everyone wants to live safely, in a clean environment that doesn't pose health risks. The government's reaction was quick and energetic. Two draft laws contained in prints 2661 and 2662 entered the Sejm on 22 June, which, after their adoption by the High Chamber, should end this period of the Polish State's inability to ensure proper waste management. These two complementary projects should once and for all put an end to the unwritten principle: the paper will withstand everything it seems to govern in waste management. The first reading of these prints took place on 27 June this year. The Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, following detailed work on the projects on 27, 28 June and 2 July, presents the reports in print Nos 2676 and 2677. First of all, the first of these prints. High Izbo! The contested law, the draft of which is contained in print 2661, will enable citizens to deal with the problem of waste dumping. The most important solution, I think, is the introduction in the added article. 48a the obligation to have claims secured for an operator operating in the field of waste management. The security will be used if necessary to cover the costs of replacement execution in enforcement proceedings incurred for the disposal and management of abandoned waste. Its height depends on the size of its operations and on the type of waste. It should be stressed that the need for safeguards is imposed by directives on the international movement of waste, on the management of used equipment and on the management of waste from the mining industry. The proposed provision contained in amendment 5 of Article. 1, ordering ownership or use of perpetual land on which waste management will be carried out, is intended to prevent waste dumping and fraudulent action by means of a method: a pole. However, it has had many controversy and difficulties in interpretation, it has been the subject of a lively discussion at a committee meeting and it will certainly be clarified by an appropriate amendment. High Izbo! The period of authorised storage of waste shall be reduced from three years to one year and the need to indicate in the administrative decision the permissible weight of simultaneously stored waste. Furthermore, there is an obligation to monitor the video location of waste storage, and this is, I think, a very important amendment, a very important modification of work. I don't think any honest businessman will be afraid of surveillance. In addition, an obligation to allow the facility to be used by the State Fire Department is imposed and specific fire protection requirements will be laid down in a regulation issued by the Minister responsible for Home Affairs, in agreement with the Minister responsible for the environment. It shall be introduced for natural and legal persons who have been convicted by a final judgment of the court or who have withdrawn the decision on waste management, so-called. the wolf ticket, i.e. the refusal to issue a decision on waste management, and the grounds for granting that refusal are clarified in detail. For large installations or waste storage sites, more than 3 000. megagrams per year, the decision will be issued by the Marshal of the voivodship, for the others - the starost. I think that this has a profound justification in the current situation, when the old men were making their decisions in no uncertain terms, which was the source of very serious environmental pollution and protests. This situation demanded a cut. In the course of the committee's work, a great deal of controversy was raised by provisions concerning the submission of declarations and non-punishment certificates and statements of a slight withdrawal of the authorisation and other statements made by the waste holder. These provisions have been clarified. They do not have to submit these statements to the shareholders. The provisions specifying the location requirements for waste management projects have also been amended in such a way as to take into account decisions on building conditions and not just local spatial planning. In addition, Article 1 is inserted. 3, including amendments to the International Waste Shipment Act, a ban on imports of mixed municipal waste and waste resulting from the treatment of municipal waste and imports of waste for disposal in the processes defined as D1 to D15 in the Annex to the Waste Directive. High Izbo! Art. 18, last, provides that the Act shall enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, with the exception of the provisions on video surveillance, which shall enter into force 6 months after the date of publication, and the provisions of the Article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 8 concerning the adoption of a regulation on fire protection requirements, which enter 12 months after the date of publication. The whole of the draft contained in the committee's report after the 20 amendments were adopted by 10 votes in favour, no votes against, with 3 abstentions and included in print No 2676. High Izbo! The government majority announced in the electoral programme that our state's inspection services would be strengthened. In particular, urgent action is required by the Environmental Protection Inspection, whose organisational structure established at the beginning of the 1990s is inefficient and completely inadequate to the significantly extended scope of its operation. I will remind you that when the inspection was created, there were about 120 procedures, now there are about 300, and therefore there was a radical extension of the scope of duties. This structure should be modified as regards the organisation and financing system. In order to improve the effectiveness of waste management control, the following solutions are proposed to strengthen and improve the work of the Environmental Protection Inspection: firstly, to enable national inspections initiated from the level of the Chief Environmental Inspector to be carried out by teams composed of inspectors from different voivodships, environmental inspectors. The true horror, which is often reported in the reports of the Supreme Audit Chamber, is the activity of groups of entrepreneurs associated with illegal practices carried out in several voivodships. In view of such waste juggling, the inspection is simply powerless. Such practices, which eliminate honest entrepreneurs from the market, can only address cross-checks. The legal and organisational basis for such control activities is provided by the provisions laid down in print draft No 2662. Secondly, it is proposed to strengthen staffing and organisational inspections through the possibility of carrying out checks on the 24-hour system, i.e. a three-shift approach to counteract infringements of environmental legislation and therefore to prevent offences or offences that pose a threat to human life or health or to the environment. This was often done on Christmas days, evenings or nights. It will now be possible to react immediately to the signs of violations. It is only immediately that the intervention checks carried out can put an end to these illegal practices that are frustrating our society. High Izbo! This, of course, requires an increase in employment and a strengthening of financial inspections, which will follow the adoption of detailed rules in this draft. Thirdly, the proposal provides for the possibility to carry out intervention checks without notification of their intention to carry them out 7 days in advance. The existing rules allowed entry without prejudice if there was a particular risk of life, health or the environment. On the other hand, because of the appeals made by companies to courts, which have usually waived the possibility of entering without prejudice, dishonest entrepreneurs have been able to break the rules for years. Examples of such impertinent mockery can be observed in the area of M.S. Warsaw. In addition to the intervention checks I am talking about, please, there are planned checks which are carried out with prejudice. There is an extension of the control powers of the Environmental Inspection inspectors when carrying out inspections to include legitimacy, interrogation, car retention, sampling, imposition of fines, use of unmanned aircraft and other competences, and to enable exploratory or operational activities to be carried out so as to obtain evidence of infringements for administrative proceedings. In the course of the inspection, the Regional Environmental Inspector will be able to issue a decision suspending the activity with immediate feasibility, so that companies, referring to the court against this decision, will not be able to delay its execution ad Kalendas Graecas, which unfortunately has so far been a permanent practice. Fourthly, the project provides for the assistance of other control or police authorities to be provided by the environmental inspectors and for cooperation with other control authorities to be extended, for example, to include educational activities in the use of the environment of other bodies and institutions. National Tax Administration, Road Transport Inspection, Border Guard, Police, Public Prosecutor's Office, judiciary, local authorities or entrepreneurs. Fifthly, the new rules introduce procedures for dealing with vehicles carrying illegal or incompatible waste. These provisions standard the rules for the transport of these vehicles to special parking lots, and also oblige the voivodships to update their waste management plans in terms of identifying places that meet the conditions for waste storage, and impose sanctions on the competent authorities, i.e. marshals and starosts, for not designating these sites. These changes finally allow enforcement of the rules on the detention of vehicles illegally transporting waste. Sixthly, the draft implements penalties for infringements of environmental regulations imposed on the basis of the Environmental Inspection Act, the Law on Forests, the Environmental Law, the Act on Vehicle Recycling, the Act on Environmental Impact Assessments, the Act on Batteries and Battery, the Act on Substances Depleting the Ozone Layer, the Act on Wasted Electrical and Electronic Equipment and the Act on Access to Genetic Resources. High Izbo! The Act will enter into force on 1 January 2019 with the exception of, first, the provision of Article. Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 concerning changes in employment relations of voivodship environmental inspection staff, which will enter into force on the date of publication. That means there's an obligation to appoint staff to move on to new tasks. Secondly, the exception to the rule as regards the date of entry into force is provisions laying down new tasks of the Environmental Protection Inspections which enter into force 14 days after the date of publication. And thirdly, the article. Point 11 (1) shall enter into force only on 1 January 2020. High Izbo! In the course of the committee's work, a number of legislative, linguistic, clarification, but also substantive amendments were introduced in the law. The entire report of the Environment Committee in print No 2677 was adopted by 10 votes with four abstentions. High Izbo! Therefore, on behalf of the committee, I ask the High House to adopt them. Thank you very much.
