I would like Mr Arthur's Rules of Procedure to state the reasons for the project. [[In one sentence: everything will be ours.]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u214	In one sentence, it'll be better, yeah. [[For who?]] Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the applicants, a group of Members, it has been my honour to present to the High House a draft law amending the Act of 5 January 2011. The draft law provides for amendments to the electoral code on the rules for the election of Members of the European Parliament in the electoral districts. The legal situation so far regarding the rules for determining the number of Members of the European Parliament representing the electoral district concerned has resulted in considerable differences in the voting potential in the various electoral districts, which could have negatively translated into turnout. In addition, the current voting conversion system favoured electoral districts with a large number of voting rights. The mechanism used was characterised by the so-called effect. a traveling mandate where the vote of voters in a given district did not have to be translated into a parliamentary mandate in that district. It is worth remembering, however, that districts in European elections usually cover the entire regions, although they have obtained sufficient and sometimes even significant support, this mandate could have been developed by another, most often larger in terms of the number of voters in the district. Thus, some districts were visited. ultimately only two mandates, and in extreme cases only one mandate for a Member of the European Parliament, resulting in a clear lack of representation of a particular region in the European Parliament. The proposed provisions change the situation described above by laying down the principle that at least three Members of the European Parliament are elected in each constituency. According to the proposal, the number of Members of the European Parliament elected in each electoral district will be determined according to the uniform norm of representation obtained by dividing the current number of voters in the country by the number of Members of the European Parliament assigned to Poland. Then, separately for each district, the current number of its voters will be divided by a previously received figure representing a uniform standard of representation. The value of the total number - before the comma - obtained in this way will mean the number of seats per electoral district. If, following this procedure, for all electoral districts, not all mandates were allocated to the districts, the remaining mandates should be allocated to those districts for which the quantities obtained reflect sequentially the highest decimals. The proposed rules for assigning mandates to individual electoral districts will allow for a real mapping of the relationship between the number of voters and the number of Members elected in a given electoral district. It will be eliminated incomprehensible to voters and an unjustified rivalry between districts for the number of seats. The way in which representatives are elected in the districts will be clear and precise, as in the case of elections to the Sejm, thus contributing to an increase in interest in the European elections, which in turn directly translates into an election turnout. In addition, the number of candidates on electoral lists will be implemented, which will now reflect the potential of the electoral district, which will result in an increase in support of the mandate of the Member. The current rules have established rigid 10-person lists of candidates in each, even the smallest in terms of the number of eligible, district, creating an imbalance in the strength of the votes cast on individual candidates. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed amendment simplifies and harmonises the electoral mechanism and makes its rules clearer, eliminates the transfer of mandates between districts, the number of mandates per constituency makes the function of a uniform standard, does not increase the cost of elections to the European Parliament. The draft law does not entail a burden on the state budget or budgets of local government units. The bill has a positive social and legal impact. The subject matter of the proposed regulation is not covered by European Union law. Thank you very much for your attention. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u215	Well, thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Andrzej Matusiewicz, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u216	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position on Parliamentary draft law amending the electoral code law of 5 January 2011, Parliamentary printing No 2660. The proposal concerns the rules for the election of Members of the European Parliament in the electoral districts. What is the current legal status in this regard? It creates significant differences in the voting potential of the different electoral districts and the voting system. He favoured electoral districts with a large number of people entitled to vote. I can give a characteristic example of the district, which consists only of the Podkarpackie voivodship, and the second constituency, the neighbouring voivodship, which consists of the Lesser Poland and Świętokrzyskie voivodships - Podkarpackie in the first two terms of office had only two Members, and this second mandate has always been threatened, it is the question of the conversion of votes. We believe that this change, the setting of a basic limit: at least three Members in the constituency, is a proper change. It will then be possible to apply the uniform standard of representation obtained by dividing the current number of voters in the country by the number of Members of the European Parliament assigned to Poland. Then - you have already told the rapporteur how this will be determined - the current number of voters in a given district will be divided by a previously received figure, which is a uniform norm of representation. The value of the total number - before the comma - obtained in this way will mean the number of seats per electoral district. This project allows for a real mapping of the relationship between the number of Members entitled to vote and the number of Members elected in the electoral district concerned. It will be eliminated incomprehensible to voters and an unjustified rivalry between districts and a constant problem why we have such and not another number of mandates. I would like to remind you that the existing rules have established rigid 10-person lists of candidates in each, even the smallest number of eligible, district, causing an imbalance in the strength of the votes cast on individual candidates. Information on the number of Members of the European Parliament elected in each electoral district will be given in the order of the President of the Republic of Poland to order elections. The amendments are therefore designed in Article. 342 electoral codes. The draft contains provisions modifying the rules for the State Election Commission to determine the results of the vote and the results of the elections. The draft repeals the provision of the Article. 358 electoral code. This provision regulates the conduct of the State Election Commission to determine the number of mandates per list of candidates. The project contains minimum vacathio legis - within 14 days of the date of publication of these provisions they will enter into force. The project is in line with European Union law, as is apparent from the opinion of the Sejm Analysis Office of 25 June 2018. The Law and Justice Club supports this project, calls for it to be given a legislative run and for further work in the Extraordinary Commission on changes in electoral law. Thank you very much. Today, our answer is a proposal to change the electoral order. We assign the number of... tickets to a certain number of voters, to a certain number of residents living in a given constituency. About 800,000. residents. What does that mean? This means that none of the regions is privileged to others. It said here that a big one has an advantage. Yes, today a large district, Silesian or Małopolska, has a definite advantage over a small district. But is the voter in a small district, the voter who lives in Lublin, Podkarpacie or Bydgoszcz the worse voter? [[What about Lublin?]] Is his vote less important than the voter who lives in Silesia? I was just... [[What if it doesn't? How's it not going?]] No. Madam, that is a great question. Imagine that in Silesia the list to the Sejm, some particular election committee, gets 20% and attendance is 20%, and in Kujawsko-Pomorskie district the list gets 25% and attendance is 25%. Where's the ticket going? I'll tell you what: Silesia. He will go to Silesia because there are more inhabitants there. And this is a bit of a violation that several Members have spoken of today. They said that the number of tickets in a given district depends on attendance. Theoretically, yes, but in the second, third step. This depends most on how many inhabitants, how many voters are in a given constituency. There was a question about election thresholds. We're not changing the electoral thresholds. We do not change the electoral thresholds, but here I agree with Mr Wolf. In the European Parliament in this ordination... I mean, there is no ruling majority in the European Parliament, there is no opposition. Those who were there, or those who are interested in it, know that there is a coalition and opposition there for every single document. On the other hand, we respect these 5% Which is, and we're not changing that. We don't change the boundaries of the electoral districts either. Many times during this debate in the last few weeks I have heard such an accusation that we will want to change the electoral districts, that if it is 51, that means there will be 17 electoral districts. It won't. The districts are as long as they were, which means there are 13 electoral districts. They're not gonna change by two miles. We are only giving the voters who live in small districts a chance to have their representatives in the European Parliament. The question was whether all voters were equal to the law. Yeah, but not everyone, according to the rule of law that is in force today, has the same chance of having their vote equal to the voter who lives in a large constituency. There was a question about the national list. I'll be honest with you, this is a solution. Indeed, in more than 20 countries of the European Union there are national, open and closed lists. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u282	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I am pleased to inform you about the activities of the Supreme Court in 2017. First, I would like to point out that the conditions under which the Supreme Court operated during the reporting period were significantly different from those in previous years. This was due to the fact that intensive work on the next Supreme Court laws began in 2017. Their result was a certain slowdown in the Supreme Court's judicial activity. However, this was not the result of negligence on the part of the Supreme Court. The work on the July 2017 Act, its adoption and then waiting for the President's signature resulted in the smooth management of the Supreme Court being impossible. In particular, this concerned the legislative activity, which in essence requires a longer period of time. The uncertainty regarding the status of Supreme Court judges and their business future has made it very difficult to appoint formations of judges, especially enlarged judges, and rapporteurs. This delayed the preparation of the papers and the appointment of the meetings. The activities of the Supreme Court in the reporting year were also affected by significant staff shortages resulting from the absence of appointments and the failure of the Minister of Justice, the Prosecutor General, to refer judges to the Supreme Court. However, despite the ruling in reduced staffing and regulatory instability of the legal system, and the impact of 11 000 cases. each year, we have managed to maintain very good efficiency in all categories of cases. The average waiting time for substantive consideration was: in the Civil Chamber - 9 months, in the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and - then - Public Affairs - 13 months, in the Criminal Chamber - 4 months, in the Military Chamber - up to 2 months from the date of the impact of the case. Thus, the average time in the Supreme Court was 7 months, which fully corresponds to the standards set out in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland and the European Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms. The efficiency of the Supreme Court is also justified by the fact that, despite the difficulties identified, compared to the previous year, the difference between the number of cases brought before it and the number of cases actually identified is more favourable. In 2016, 325 cases were considered more than received and 377 more cases were identified in the reporting year 2017. A total of 11 373 cases were identified in 2017, including 8034 cassation and cassation complaints, 1146 complaints, 147 legal issues. During the reporting year, a special event was the celebration of the 100th anniversary of the Supreme Court. The assessment of the activities of the highest judicial court throughout the century required not only an in-depth history study, but also a summary of the rich judicial achievements and commemoration of outstanding figures deserved for the justice system and for Poland. Two monographs on the history of the Supreme Court were prepared on the occasion of the Jubilee, including the monograph entitled "The Supreme Court." The President, Judges and Prosecutors of the Supreme Court 1917-2017 by Professor Bereza and a supplement to the Jubilee Book of the 90th anniversary of the Supreme Court.History and Modernity, covering the years 2007-2017, by the Judge of the Supreme Court of Wiesław Kozieewicz. An occasional installation was also carried out outside and inside the Supreme Court's premises and an exhibition entitled: The 100th anniversary of the Supreme Court and the General Court. The main jubilee celebrations preceded the meeting of the judges of the District Court in Piotrków Trybunalski devoted to the memory of the first President of the Supreme Court of Stanisław Pomian-Srzednicki. It took place on 1 September 2017. The official event inaugurated a meeting of judges, retired judges and Supreme Court staff on 27 September at the Supreme Court. During this ceremony, commemorative medals and scientific publications were presented on the occasion of the Jubilee. The second part of the ceremony was preceded by the Holy Mass in the Architedraal Basilica under the invitation of the Martyrdom of St. John the Baptist in Warsaw and then the ceremonies moved to the Royal Castle, where the presidents were present, representatives of the highest state institutions, courts, tribunals, universities, professional authorities, organizations and associations, as well as judges and employees of the Supreme Court. It was a special event that will be remembered for a long time by all the judges and employees of the Supreme Court. However, the most important was, of course, the current activity, especially in the area of smooth judic supervision. The case-law of each Chamber, detailed in the following section of the information, which is contained in the book that you have received, concerned a very diverse group of issues. This was the result of considerable legal instability and the entire normative system, including numerous amendments and the emergence of completely new laws requiring urgent judicial interpretation. For this reason, interpretation resolutions took on particular importance. In addition to harmonising their views, they not only shaped the practice of courts but also influenced the functioning of law enforcement agencies, lawyers and lawyers. Legislative activities also have an important impact on ensuring legal certainty and enhancing citizens' confidence in the state. The increasing complexity of the legal problems examined has led the Supreme Court to refer repeatedly to international law, European law, the case law of the ECHR and the Court of Justice of the European Union. The subject matter of legal matters decided by the Supreme Court was very diverse in the same way as in previous years. Specific issues are presented in this broad, comprehensive annual information. I will skip these specific issues and draw attention to a number of decisions that were pro-Citizen-like in order to ensure the protection of economically vulnerable participants in legal proceedings and trade, as well as the protection of the social interest. Within the scope of the largest chamber of the Supreme Court, the Civil Chamber, it is possible to refer to Resolution III of the CZP 11/17, which protects existing housing relations in the event of apparent legislative shortcomings. The case-law of the Civil Chamber of the Supreme Court also raised legal issues concerning the amended 2003 Act on the formation of the agricultural system, which were particularly important to constitutional standards. The doubts raised in the case law of the general courts have revealed a number of legislative errors made by the legislator, in particular concerning inter-temporal law and the harmonious position of changes made in the entire regulatory context. The Supreme Court in the Civil Chamber also dealt with issues related to the major issue of reprivatisation. In the resolution of the composition of the seven judges, CZP III 84/16, in fact, covering the circumstances closely linked to the action of the 1946 Decree, referred to the issue whether the owner of the self-employed property who gave it to a dependent tenant was liable to the owner in respect of the claim for compensation for the use of the property, even though he did not actually own it. In order to settle this issue, the Supreme Court stressed that the failure to approve the reprivatization law forces the courts to assess the claims of those who were subject to nationalisation after the war, taking into account the applicable law. The resolution III CZP 44/17, which addresses the problem of the estate curator not covered by the property owner, also deserves attention. The problem was whether such a curator could represent a decrease in the proceedings for the abolition of joint ownership of immovable property with the effect of unknown heirs or co-owners. It is very important that the Supreme Court gives a negative answer to this question, especially in the face of a number of cases involving the abolition of joint ownership of immovable property which the joint owners or their heirs are not familiar with. In such cases, it is usually the property abandoned in connection with World War II and the Holocaust. However, the greatest difficulties of interpretation arose in procedural law. Enforcement and judicial costs remain particularly important from the point of view of citizens' interests. For many years, this issue has been the subject of interpretation resolutions by the Supreme Court. For a few years I have been paying attention to this. This is worrying because the doubts concern specific standards, which are largely technical in nature. Those provisions should therefore be precise and unambiguous. However, the Supreme Court has repeatedly had to resolve the problems that should be resolved by the legislator's decision. In the case-law of the Criminal Chamber, matters in the field of substantive criminal law were dominated. Other cases concerned the process, bordering on the issues of the system and the process, inter-temporal issues, Union law, fiscal criminal law and enforcement criminal law. Intertemporal issues and interpretation of new criminal laws were more common in decisions issued outside the legislative procedure. In general, this intertemporality is weakly noticeable in legislation and poorly worked out by the legislator. In the area of substantive criminal law and Union law, the resolution of the composition of seven judges on the issue of criminal liability for gambling contrary to the requirements of the Gambling Act was important. It's coming back, and I talked about it last year, too. The Supreme Court held that the conflict of national law with Union law, in the light of the principle of direct application of European Union law, may lead to the replacement of national legislation by Union regulation or to the exclusion of national law by a directly effective standard of Union law. The resolution of the composition of the seven judges, I KZP 4/17, issued following the examination of the legal issue referred to by the Supreme Court, was very important. The Supreme Court has ruled that the law of grace, as the President's power, is laid down in Article. The first sentence of the Constitution, 139th sentence, can only be enforced against persons whose wines are found to be a valid sentence. The resolution of seven Supreme Court Judges, I KZP 2/17, which was issued after the hearing of the Ombudsman's application, was important for the practice of applying the conditional institution of early dismissal. The Supreme Court stated that the criteria set out in Article are the basis for ruling on conditional early release from imprisonment. 77, on the other hand, are not grounds for deciding on this directive the extent of the penalties referred to in Article. Article 53 and Article 53 54. However, a separate sentence was submitted to the resolution. Among the judgments issued outside the legislative procedure, special attention should be paid to the decision [[♪ The bell ♪]] the issues that have arisen...

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u514	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! This is an amendment very important from the point of view of the sovereignty of Polish universities. The point of this amendment is that Polish universities should not be helpless in these disputes. The public university, creating a capital company with a private, foreign entity, is covered by the law resulting from international agreements, for example. CETA. In the event of a conflict, the university is subject to the jurisdiction of arbitration courts in the country of the investor. It can be assumed that, in addition to the cumbersome and high costs of such proceedings, the court will be governed by different rules from those provided for in our Polish legal order, including the Constitution. The scientific policy of the Polish state will not be taken into account. So if you want the sovereignty of Polish universities, vote in favour [[♪ The bell ♪]] that amendment. [[Applause]]

[[Voice from the room: Yes, sir!]] Technical universities, artistic universities, state-owned vocational schools have supported this project because it introduces separate funding mechanisms for them. In addition, there is support for employers' organisations. You will find all this on our website if you would like to look at it and see what the consultation process has looked like, which lasted over 2 years and took meetings with the crowds, thousands of representatives of the academic community. As for the allegations that have been made here, it is about the substantive or rather not substantive ones, I would like to address them one at a time. [[Cocky little brat.]] Increasing autonomy, the question of university autonomy. The Act increases the autonomy of the university by increasing its organisational freedom within. It is the academic community that decides how the university works inside, not the law. The law does not impose this, and this is precisely the extension of university organisational autonomy, not the imposition of structure from the top in the law. As far as issues related to the evaluation of science are concerned, we are moving away from the mechanism of punktosis, which was left to us after the previous law. So far, scientists have had to collect points for mass, not to wonder whether they were publishing in a reasonable way and whether they could face ambitious research challenges. Now, as a rule, a scientific worker will present his four most important scientific achievements and thus be able to focus on real scientific research and pursue his ambitions, not collect points. In addition, in the law itself, we guarantee that we take into account the specifics of humanities and social sciences, strengthening the importance of monographs in the evaluation of scientific research. What is also important and what is the request of trade unions introduced by this law is that we stabilise the issue of employment in universities, in particular contracts are more quickly transformed into those for an indefinite period. There is a possibility of job promotions, which means that there is no need to carry out another competition if there is a promotion to the next post after obtaining another degree of science or meeting the requirements that will be indicated in the statutes. First of all, it is also a revolutionary change in the education of future scientific elites, i.e. the creation of doctoral schools. So far, PhD students in most cases have been poor, let's face it. For the first time, the Act introduces a mechanism for a universal scholarship system. Every doctoral student recruited since 2019/2020 will receive a scholarship, will be covered by maternity or father leave. This is a policy that, when it comes to scientists, also strengthens family issues. That hasn't happened yet. As far as student cases are concerned, I am very sorry that there has been such a statement here that students are only for a moment. ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u573	Mr Krzysztof Szulowski, Law and Justice. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u574	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On 24 June 2018 in Ryki, Lublin Voivodeship, celebrations were held to commemorate, on the 72nd anniversary of his death, the memory of Major. Mariana Bernaciaka nicknamed Orlik. The organizers of the ceremony were the mayor of Ryk Jarosław Żakzek and the World Union of Soldiers of the National Army in Ryki. They participated in it, among others. Major's family, combatants, numerous flag mails, head of the Office for Combatants and Repressed Persons Jan Józef Kasprzyk, Senator Stanisław Gogacz, Krzysztof Szulowski, director of the Department of the Institute of National Remembrance in Lublin Marcin Krzysztofik, representative of Lublin Voivodeship Mariusz Wicik, local government officials, representatives of a number of institutions, residents of Ryk and surroundings and guests. Major Marian Bernaciak is a hero of the anti-communist post-war underground, an outstanding guerrilla leader in the area not only of Lublin and Mazowsze, but of the entire Polish state. As commander of the guerrilla group and conspiracy structures of the Puławy Province of AK-WiN, one of the best organized in the Lublin district, he made a significant contribution to the fight for the liberation of Poland from German occupation and then Soviet occupation. He was a member of the 1939 defence war in ranks 2. The Heavy Artillery regiment from Chelm. During Włodzimierz Wołyński's defense, he got into Soviet captivity, from which he fled and returned to his hometown. In 1940, he became involved in conspiracy activities. He formed a guerrilla unit composed of 15. The Infantry Regiment of the National Army, which carried out more than 20 combat actions against Germany in 1944. During the action, in 1944 Ryki was taken by Ryki himself, and in Dęblin he saved important military, economic facilities from being destroyed and saved the population from being exported. In 1944, at the head of some 350 guerrillas, he marched to help the fighting Warsaw insurgents who failed. After the disbandment, he was hiding, wanted by the NKVD. In 1945, he reconstructed the branch and became the commander of the group of troops of the Association of Freedom and Independence in the District Inspector of Puławy, and then Deputy Inspector of Puławy for Security Affairs. This group carried out many actions against the communist authorities. In April 1945, Orlik carried out one of the most bravo actions, breaking up a county prison in Puławy in Ubek. On 1 May 1945 the branch of Orlika entered the market in Kock and turned the communist rally into a manifestation of support for the Polish Government in exile. The largest guerrilla battle of the anti-communist underground after the war was fought by Orlik troops on 24 May 1945 in the Stock Forest near Kazimierz Dolny, when they were unexpectedly attacked by a strong operating group of NKVD, KBW, UB and MO. On July 27, 1945, the Orlika branch freed from rail transport near Bąkowce, near Dęblin, about 120 prisoners, including many soldiers of the underworld of independence. On 24 June 1946, Major Orlik was attacked by a group of UB and MO soldiers and officers near the village of Piotrówek, Mazowieckie Voivodeship. Twice wounded trying to escape, he died from gunshot wounds. Like many of the steadfast soldiers, he was buried in an unknown place because he was to be erased from national memory. Patriotic and religious ceremonies in Ryki were held under the national patronage of the President of the Republic of Poland Andrzej Duda. The event began with Mass. in the Church of St. The most holy Savior in Ryki with the participation of a military honorary aide, during which the Silver Cross of the Order of War of Virtuti Militari was placed as a vote. Marian Bernaciak, Orlik in 1945. The place of the ceremony was special. It was in this church that, after the tragic death of Orlik in 1946, residents gathered in spite of the bans and persecutions at the funeral service at an empty coffin. Then the Chamber of Remembrance was solemnly opened. Mariana Bernaciak, Orlika, in the seat of the Municipal and Municipal Centre for Culture in Ryki, over which the honorary patronage was taken by the Institute of National Remembrance Branch in Lublin. The event ended with a patriotic concert. Hello and praise the heroes! Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-07-03-sejm-66-1.u578	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! A whole series of protests about the new EU directive, which is about to be adopted, has recently gone through Poland. copyright protection, referred to by an analogy by ACTA 2 Directive. This directive is absolutely unacceptable and it is outrageous at all that someone wants to implement such a directive. Firstly, let us be honest that it will not defend copyright, because the method adopted in this directive is absolutely not suitable for this. The concept of copyright that is being tried to apply, that is, this traditional concept of copyright, was created when there were analog media. During digital media and virtual reality, this is absolutely not suitable for copyright protection to be applied to content posted on the Internet and cannot actually be applied effectively. It's a little bit like we're trying to fix a computer program with a hammer or a screwdriver. It's just impossible. Secondly, no one disputes that copyright is to be protected. It's obvious. The creators should be paid for his work, the author of the work should be paid for his hard work, for his creation. But here we need completely new solutions and completely different, according to a completely different philosophy created, keeping up with the technical development and the reality that we now have. Thirdly, finally, and this is the most outrageous thing, in fact, this directive introduces no less, no more, through the obligation to control users' content, preventive censorship. In a civilized world in many constitutions, including ours, preventive censorship is forbidden. It is unimaginable why the authors of this project and the MEPs voting in favour of this project have failed to take into account the fact that they support preventive censorship. Therefore, a simple question arises: Why has this new concept emerged? ACTA 2, why would someone even think of such an idea, why would they even have this idea, why would it serve? Ladies and gentlemen, let us not be fooled, the media is the fourth power, while the weapon of this fourth power is information. This information is precisely the tool by which the fourth power functions, functions and is effective. Whoever controls the information controls the fourth power and at the same time part of the power in general. Whoever controls the information also has control over who comes to power, who exercises it, etc., etc. For this reason, governments have always - always - done their best to control and influence the flow of information. Governments hate this situation when they have no control over the flow of information, and they will always try to do something to limit or control this flow. This cannot be accepted, and for this reason I appeal to all those who will hear these words to take part in the second part of these protests. July 13th will be the second wave of protests against ACTA 2. There is a need for a large presence, because only before a large amount, only before the power is given way. Power is only afraid of strength and it is necessary to show strength, you have to show big agreement, large cooperation, a large number of people who will protest loudly and unambiguously against this new concept. They protest against restricting our freedom, protest against restricting freedom on the Internet, protest against restricting freedom of expression, protest against prevention censorship, protest against restricting our freedom and freedom of expression. So I invite you all, on July 13th you must not miss there, especially young people. It's our duty. It is our duty to protect our freedom and to oppose all attempts at its artificial and improper restraint by means of ineffective means. Besides, I think the U.S.S.S. should be destroyed. Thank you very much.
